# Daily delivery-step audit: 2026-11-02 UTC

Window: **2026-11-02T00:00:00.000Z ≤ event time < 2026-11-03T00:00:00.000Z**.
Source: a SQLite backup of the controller's durable `goal.sqlite`, captured at
2026-11-03T00:23:13.693738Z; extraction uses retained source `7129bd8d2203ef3afdc2bf398db69442c3044789`.
The recommendation is to **make landing state and telemetry one idempotent transition**. The current reader
omits all 1,091 successful dev landings from its landing-duration sample because `closed` precedes `finished`.

## Daily flow

```sh
bun tools/goal/src/cli.ts report --flow --since 2026-11-02T00:00:00.000Z --json
```

The complete JSON response is included below, with whitespace compacted. The real CLI `run` entry point was
invoked against the private backup through runtime injection, avoiding runtime initialization in the read-only
main checkout. Its lifecycle clock is `2026-11-03T00:25:21.349Z`. This is telemetry extraction, not acceptance.
`--since` has no upper bound: the raw response includes November 3 events and capture-time task states.
The figures in this audit instead use `readFlow` filtered at **both** boundaries and `flowReport` at midnight;
current terminal states are omitted from that historical calculation. No historical state is invented.

| Flow interval | Completed pairs | Sum of recorded intervals | p90 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Dispatch → start request | 0 | unknown | unknown |
| Start request → running receipt | 2,218 | 230.425 s | 95 ms |
| Check request → release | 0 | unknown | unknown |
| Worker started → finished | 2,135 | 527.056 h | 33.179 min |
| Landing enqueued → finished | 400 | 81.340 h | 45.586 min |
| Dev update / verification / integration / promotion | 0 each | unknown | unknown |

Intervals are overlapping residency or queue-plus-execution, not CPU time; unmatched and cross-midnight
intervals contribute no duration. Landing durations cover 387 failed outcomes and 13 successful non-dev outcomes,
excluding every successful dev landing. Repeated enqueue events can replace interval starts. Missing check-slot
telemetry also prevents separating worker implementation, check execution and waiting costs. All five recorded
queues are empty at midnight; missing producers mean actual check, verification and promotion queues are unknown.
The bounded reader reports efficiency 0.5122965807760267 and 1.5833333333333333 worker launches per integrated task;
these are incomplete-telemetry ratios, not productive efficiency or distinct attempts per delivery.

## Steps, catches and costs

**Allowance cost is unknown for every step.** Pool observations and aggregate tokens do not attribute allowance
consumption to stages. Prevented-defect counts are unknown unless stated as a recorded rejection below; retries,
failed infrastructure and repeated observations are not independent defects. Time is unknown except where stated.
`hostMs` below is the recorded landing envelope grouped by its terminal outcome, not isolated substep execution;
a successful outcome's envelope includes earlier setup and guards. Do not add it to the flow intervals above.

## One structural change and its measure

The control-plane owner should make queue claim and terminal state publication write canonical flow transitions
in the same SQLite transaction, keyed by landing identity and execution generation. Emit enqueue once; emit start
on claim rather than queue enumeration; emit successful finish before terminal cleanup closes outstanding work.
Retain cancellation/stopping closure as a distinct unsuccessful outcome. Existing guards and gates remain required.
This is a recommendation for the Bun/SQLite controller, not an adopted owner-document change or copied code.

Today, queue enumeration emits **29,639 starts for 1,516 task/attempt pairs**, both the store and queue emit enqueue,
and all **1,091** dev completions have a preceding landing `closed`. The reader discards those open intervals.
Compare the next complete UTC day after adoption with this baseline: the primary measure is successful-dev-landing
interval coverage, **0/1,091 today → 100% of eligible same-day completions**, with exactly one canonical enqueue,
claim and terminal outcome per execution. Track unmatched cross-midnight intervals separately. Also report event
writes per actual execution and p90 successful landing latency; today's successful-dev p90 and allowance savings
are unknown. A lower event count alone does not establish faster delivery or allowance savings.

## Primary sources and shared pattern

- Controller telemetry: `flow_events`, `worker_events`, `dev_verifier_runs`, `dev_verifier_findings` and
  `lifecycle_metric_emissions` in the captured database. Daily event/outcome queries use `at >= start AND at < end`;
  verifier rows use `created_at`, hourly rows `hour_bucket`. Gate catches group failed `gate-outcome` bodies by
  `stage`; each stage's rejection count also equals its distinct task/attempt count. All daily figures above
  derive from persisted rows, not process logs, inferred retries, prior audit prose or estimates.
- Retained controller source (AGPL-3.0-only, `tools/goal/package.json`):
  [queue writers](../src/land.ts#L124), [store enqueue](../src/store.ts#L1690),
  [terminal closure](../src/flow.ts#L203), [interval reader](../src/flow.ts#L120),
  [report handler](../src/worker-commands.ts#L237), and [delivery rules](../README.md#integration-promotion-culprit-isolation-and-attempt-fencing).
  Queue insertion already has a unique task/attempt key; its telemetry writes do not share that idempotence.
- Reused Temporal clone: `.reference/delivery-tooling/temporal/`, commit
  `3eb5fb80523443d8583bcec463843275908f1bbc`, MIT (`LICENSE`). Its
  [workflow mutation](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/3eb5fb80523443d8583bcec463843275908f1bbc/service/history/workflow/mutable_state_impl.go#L7756)
  returns state, generated tasks and conditional record version alongside transaction history;
  [event preparation](https://github.com/temporalio/temporal/blob/3eb5fb80523443d8583bcec463843275908f1bbc/service/history/workflow/mutable_state_impl.go#L8746)
  binds history to workflow/run and transaction identities. No clone code was run.

The shared pattern is a durable identity and conditional state transition. Applying that pattern to telemetry
would make observations describe the same transition as delivery, while preserving separate retry generations.
